\displaystyle\sum_{vw\in E(\mathbb{G})}\sigma^{(\alpha_{1})}(v)\sigma^{(\alpha_{1})}\left({w}\right)+\tau^{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\rho)}\left({v}\right)\tau^{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\rho)}\left({w}\right)

\displaystyle=2{\mathcal{E}}(\psi).

(6.34)

Furthermore, the number of possible graphs with said number of edges as prescribed by
\psi
equals

\displaystyle\prod_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\binom{\binom{\rho_{2}}{2}}{\psi_{s}}\prod_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\binom{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}{\psi_{\{s,t\}}}.

(6.35)

Since the total number of graphs with
m
edges equals
\binom{\binom{n}{2}}{m}
, the assertion follows from (
6.33
), (
6.34
) and (
6.35
).
∎

Lemma 6.10
.

We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)^{2}]\leq(1+o(1))

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathfrak{B},\rho\in\mathfrak{R}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}),\psi\in\Psi}\exp\left({2\beta{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)}\right)\binom{n}{\rho_{++},\ldots,\rho_{--}}\binom{m}{\psi}\prod_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\left({\frac{{\rho_{s}\choose 2}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{s}}\prod_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\left({\frac{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{\{s,t\}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\exp\left({\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s\}}(\psi_{\{s\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}(\rho_{s}-1)}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s,t\}}(\psi_{\{s,t\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}}\right).

Proof.

We rewrite several of the binomial coefficients from the expression from Lemma
6.9
in terms of falling factorials.
First,

\displaystyle\binom{\binom{n}{2}}{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m!}\binom{n}{2}_{m}=\frac{1}{m!}\binom{n}{2}^{m}\prod_{j=1}^{m-1}\frac{\binom{n}{2}-j}{\binom{n}{2}}=\frac{1}{m!}\binom{n}{2}^{m}\exp\left({-\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}\frac{j}{\binom{n}{2}}+o(1)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m!}\binom{n}{2}^{m}\exp\left({-\binom{m}{2}\binom{n}{2}^{-1}+o(1)}\right)=\frac{1}{m!}\binom{n}{2}^{m}\exp\left({-\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}+o(1)}\right).

(6.36)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\binom{\binom{\rho_{s}}{2}}{\psi_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\psi_{s}!}\binom{\rho_{s}}{2}^{\psi_{s}}\exp\left({-\frac{\psi_{s}(\psi_{s}-1)}{\rho_{s}(\rho_{s}-1)}+o(1)}\right),

(6.37)

\displaystyle\binom{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}{\psi_{\{s,t\}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\psi{\{s,t\}}!}(\rho_{s}\rho_{t})^{\psi_{\{s,t\}}}\exp\left({-\frac{\psi_{\{s,t\}}(\psi_{\{s,t\}}-1)}{2\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}}\right).

(6.38)

Combining (
6.36
)–(
6.38
) with Lemma
6.9
yields the desired bound.
∎

Lemma 6.11
.

Given
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\rho
let

\displaystyle\psi^{*}_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{\zeta^{*}}\left({\frac{\rho_{s}\exp(\beta)}{n}}\right)^{2},\qquad\psi^{*}_{\{s,t\}}=\frac{2m\rho_{s}\rho_{t}\exp(-2\beta\mathbb{1}\{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{-1}\})}{\zeta^{*}n^{2}},\qquad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\zeta^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\left({\frac{\rho_{s}\exp(\beta)}{n}}\right)^{2}+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\frac{2\rho_{s}\rho_{t}\exp(-2\beta\mathbb{1}\{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{-1}\})}{n^{2}}.

Moreover, let

\displaystyle\Psi^{*}=\Psi^{*}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\rho)=\left\{{\psi\in\Psi:\|\psi-\psi^{*}\|\leq\sqrt{n}\log n}\right\}.

Then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)^{2}]\leq(1+o(1))

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathfrak{B},\rho\in\mathfrak{R}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}),\psi\in\Psi^{*}}\exp\left({2\beta{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)}\right)\binom{n}{\rho_{++},\ldots,\rho_{--}}\binom{m}{\psi}\prod_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\left({\frac{{\rho_{s}\choose 2}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{s}}\prod_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\left({\frac{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{\{s,t\}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\exp\left({\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s\}}(\psi_{\{s\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}(\rho_{s}-1)}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s,t\}}(\psi_{\{s,t\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}}\right).

Proof.

For given
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathfrak{B}
,
\rho\in\mathfrak{R}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
and
\psi\in\Psi
let

\displaystyle Y(\psi)

\displaystyle=\exp\left({2\beta{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)}\right)\binom{m}{\psi}\prod_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\left({\frac{{\rho_{s}\choose 2}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{s}}\prod_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\left({\frac{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{\{s,t\}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\exp\left({\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s\}}(\psi_{\{s\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}(\rho_{s}-1)}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s,t\}}(\psi_{\{s,t\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}}\right).

be the contribution of
\psi
to the second moment formula from Lemma
6.10
.
To estimate
Y(\psi)
let

\displaystyle q_{s}

\displaystyle=\psi_{s}/m,\quad q_{s,t}=\psi_{\{s,t\}}/m,\quad p_{s}=\psi_{s}^{*}/m,\quad p_{\{s,t\}}=\psi_{\{s,t\}}^{*}/m.

Furthermore, set

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}q_{s}\log\frac{q_{s}}{p_{s}}+\sum_{s,t\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}q_{\{s,t\}}\log\frac{q_{\{s,t\}}}{p_{\{s,t\}}}.

Applying Stirling’s formula, we find

\displaystyle Y(\psi)

\displaystyle=n^{O(1)}\exp\left({-m\mathcal{D}(\psi)+m\log\zeta^{*}}\right).

Furthermore, the expression
\mathcal{D}(\psi)
is just the Kullback-Leibler divergence of the probability distributions
(q_{s})_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
and
(p_{s})_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
.
Since the Kullback-Leibler divergence is strictly convex and takes its minimum of zero at the point
q_{s}=p_{s}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}Y(\psi)

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{\psi\in\Psi^{*}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\rho)}Y(\psi),

which implies the assertion.
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
\mathfrak{R}^{*}=\{\rho\in\{0,\ldots,n\}:|\rho-n/4|\leq n^{3/4}\log^{4}n\}
.
Then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)^{2}]\leq(1+o(1))

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathfrak{B},\rho\in\mathfrak{R}^{*},\psi\in\Psi^{*}}\exp\left({2\beta{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)}\right)\binom{n}{\rho_{++},\ldots,\rho_{--}}\binom{m}{\psi}\prod_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\left({\frac{{\rho_{s}\choose 2}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{s}}\prod_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\left({\frac{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{\{s,t\}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\exp\left({\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s\}}(\psi_{\{s\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}(\rho_{s}-1)}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s,t\}}(\psi_{\{s,t\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}}\right).

Proof.

We expand the r.h.s. of the bound on the second moment from Lemma
6.11
to the leading order.
To this end, let

\displaystyle\gamma_{++}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{--}=\rho/n,

\displaystyle\gamma_{+-}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{-+}=\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{++}

and define

\displaystyle\varphi(\gamma_{++})

\displaystyle=-2\gamma_{++}\log\gamma_{++}-2\gamma_{+-}\log\gamma_{+-}+\frac{m}{n}\log\left({2(\gamma_{++}^{2}+\gamma_{+-}^{2})\exp(2\beta)+2(\gamma_{++}^{2}+\gamma_{+-}^{2})\exp(-2\beta)+8\gamma_{++}\gamma_{+-}}\right)

\displaystyle=-2\gamma_{++}\log\gamma_{++}-2\gamma_{+-}\log\gamma_{+-}+\frac{m}{n}\log\left[{\operatorname{ch}(2\beta)+8\left({2\gamma_{++}^{2}-\gamma_{++}}\right)\operatorname{sh}^{2}\beta}\right].

We obtain the following bound on the contribution of the
\rho
-summand for any given
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathfrak{B}
and any
\psi\in\Psi^{*}
:

\displaystyle S(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\rho,\psi)

\displaystyle=\exp\left({2\beta{\mathcal{E}}(\psi)+\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s\}}(\psi_{\{s\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}(\rho_{s}-1)}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\frac{\psi_{\{s,t\}}(\psi_{\{s,t\}}-1)}{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\binom{n}{\rho_{++},\ldots,\rho_{--}}\binom{m}{\psi}\prod_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\left({\frac{{\rho_{s}\choose 2}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{s}}\prod_{\{s,t\}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{-1}}\left({\frac{\rho_{s}\rho_{t}}{\binom{n}{2}}}\right)^{\psi_{\{s,t\}}}

\displaystyle=\binom{n}{\rho_{++},\ldots,\rho_{--}}\exp(m\log\zeta^{*}+O(\sqrt{n}\log^{2}n))=\exp(n\varphi(\gamma_{++})+O(\sqrt{n}\log^{2}n)).

(6.39)

We are going to show that the function
\varphi
attains its maximum at
\gamma_{++}\sim 1/4
.
Indeed, the derivatives of
\varphi
come out as

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma_{++})

\displaystyle=2(\log(1/2-\gamma_{++})-\log\gamma_{++})+\frac{m}{n}\cdot\frac{8(4\gamma_{++}-1)\operatorname{sh}^{2}\beta}{\operatorname{ch}(2\beta)+8\left({2\gamma_{++}^{2}-\gamma_{++}}\right)\operatorname{sh}^{2}\beta},

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime\prime}(\gamma_{++})

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{\gamma_{++}(1-2\gamma_{++})}+\frac{m}{n}\cdot\frac{32\operatorname{sh}^{2}\beta}{\operatorname{ch}(2\beta)+8\left({2\gamma_{++}^{2}-\gamma_{++}}\right)\operatorname{sh}^{2}\beta}-\frac{m}{n}\cdot\left({\frac{8(4\gamma_{++}-1)\operatorname{sh}^{2}\beta}{\operatorname{ch}(2\beta)+8\left({2\gamma_{++}^{2}-\gamma_{++}}\right)\operatorname{sh}^{2}\beta}}\right)^{2}.

Hence, under the assumption
d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta<1
the function
\varphi(\gamma_{++})
is strictly concave and attains its maximum at
\gamma_{++}=1/4
.
Therefore, the assertion follows from (
6.39
).
∎

Corollary 6.13
.

We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle[Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)^{2}]\leq(1+o(1))2^{-m}n^{-2m}\exp\left({\frac{m}{n}+{m^{2}\over n^{2}}\left({1-\frac{4\operatorname{ch}^{2}(2\beta)}{\operatorname{ch}^{2}\beta}}\right)}\right)\Sigma^{\otimes},\qquad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\otimes}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathfrak{B}\\
\rho\in\mathfrak{R}^{*}\end{subarray}}\binom{n}{\rho_{++},\ldots,\rho_{--}}\left({\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}{\rho_{s}\choose 2}\exp(2\beta)+(\rho_{++}+\rho_{--})(\rho_{+-}+\rho_{-+})+\left({\rho_{++}\rho_{--}+\rho_{+-}\rho_{-+}}\right)\exp(-2\beta)}\right)^{m}.

Proof.

This follows by combining Lemmas
6.11
and
6.12
with Stirling’s formula.
∎

Lemma 6.14
.

We have

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\otimes}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{4^{n}n^{2m-3/2}}{\left({1-d\operatorname{th}\beta}\right)\sqrt{1-d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta}}\exp\left({-{16m\over n}+{m\over n}\cdot\log\left({\exp\left({2\beta}\right)+\exp\left({-2\beta}\right)+2\over 8}\right)}\right).

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle f(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)

\displaystyle=h(\gamma_{1})+\gamma_{1}h(\delta/\gamma_{1})+(1-\gamma_{1})h((\gamma_{2}-\delta)/(1-\gamma_{1}))+\frac{m}{n}\log g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta),\qquad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)

\displaystyle=4\left({\operatorname{ch}(2\beta)-1}\right)\delta^{2}+2\left({\operatorname{ch}(2\beta)-1}\right)\left({1-2\gamma_{1}-2\gamma_{2}}\right)\delta

\displaystyle\qquad+\exp\left({2\beta}\right)\left({\gamma_{1}^{2}+\gamma_{2}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}+1/2}\right)+\exp\left({-2\beta}\right)\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{1}^{2}-\gamma_{2}^{2}+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}-2\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}.

These functions satisfy

\displaystyle g(1/2,1/2,1/4)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{ch}^{2}\beta/2,

\displaystyle f(1/2,1/2,1/4)

\displaystyle=2\log 2+\frac{m}{n}\log\frac{\operatorname{ch}^{2}\beta}{2}.

Using Stirling’s formula, we obtain

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\otimes}

\displaystyle\sim{4\sqrt{2}\over(\pi n)^{3/2}}n^{2m}\exp\left({-{m\over n}\cdot{4\exp\left({2\beta}\right)\over(\exp(\beta)+\exp(-\beta))^{2}}}\right)\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathfrak{B},\,\rho\in\mathfrak{R}^{*}}\exp\left({nf\left({{\alpha_{1}\over n},{\alpha_{2}\over n},{\rho_{++}\over n}}\right)}\right).

(6.40)

We are going to evaluate the sum by way of the Laplace method.
Thus, we calculate the first and second derivatives of
f
.
The first derivatives work out to be

\displaystyle{\partial f\over\partial\gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle=\log\left({1-\gamma_{1}-{\gamma_{2}+\delta}}\right)-\log\left({\gamma_{1}-{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle+{m\over n}{-2\delta\left({\exp\left({\beta}\right)-\exp\left({-\beta}\right)}\right)^{2}+2\gamma_{1}\exp\left({2\beta}\right)+\gamma_{2}\exp\left({2\beta}\right)+\gamma_{2}\exp\left({-2\beta}\right)-2\gamma_{1}-2\gamma_{2}+1-\exp\left({2\beta}\right)\over g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)},

\displaystyle{\partial f\over\partial\gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle=\log\left({1-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}+\delta}\right)-\log\left({\gamma_{2}-\delta}\right),

\displaystyle+{m\over n}{-2\delta\left({\exp\left({\beta}\right)-\exp\left({-\beta}\right)}\right)^{2}+2\gamma_{2}\exp\left({2\beta}\right)+\gamma_{1}\exp\left({-2\beta}\right)+\gamma_{1}\exp\left({2\beta}\right)-2\gamma_{2}-2\gamma_{1}+1-\exp\left({2\beta}\right)\over g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)},

\displaystyle{\partial f\over\partial\delta}

\displaystyle=\log(\gamma_{1}-\delta)-\log\delta+\log\left({\gamma_{2}-\delta}\right)-\log\left({1-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}+\delta}\right)

\displaystyle+{m\over n}{4\left({\exp\left({\beta}\right)-\exp\left({-\beta}\right)}\right)^{2}\delta+\left({\exp\left({\beta}\right)-\exp\left({-\beta}\right)}\right)^{2}\left({1-2\gamma_{1}-2\gamma_{2}}\right)\over g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)}.

Hence, the Jacobian satisfies

\displaystyle Df\big|_{\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=1/2,\,\delta=1/4}

\displaystyle=0.

(6.41)

Further, for the second derivatives we obtain

\displaystyle{\partial^{2}f\over\partial\gamma_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-{1\over 1-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}+\delta}-{1\over\gamma_{1}-\delta}+{\frac{m}{n}}\cdot{\left({2\exp\left({2\beta}\right)-2}\right)\cdot g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)-\left({\partial g/\partial\gamma_{1}}\right)^{2}\over g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)^{2}},

\displaystyle{\partial^{2}f\over\partial\gamma_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-{1\over 1-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}+\delta}-{1\over\gamma_{2}-\delta}+{\frac{m}{n}}\cdot{\left({2\exp\left({2\beta}\right)-2}\right)\cdot g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)-(\partial g/\partial\gamma_{2})^{2}\over g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)^{2}},

\displaystyle{\partial^{2}f\over\partial\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle=-{1\over\gamma_{1}-\delta}-{1\over\gamma_{2}-\delta}-{1\over\delta}-{1\over 1-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}+\delta}+{\frac{m}{n}}\cdot{\left({4\exp\left({2\beta}\right)+4\exp\left({-2\beta}\right)-8}\right)\cdot g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)-(\partial g/\partial\delta)^{2}\over g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)^{2}},

\displaystyle{\partial^{2}f\over\partial\gamma_{1}\partial\gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle=-{1\over 1-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}+\delta}+{\frac{m}{n}}\cdot{\left({\exp\left({-2\beta}\right)+\exp\left({2\beta}\right)-2}\right)\cdot g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)-(\partial g/\partial\gamma_{1})(\partial g/\partial\gamma_{2})\over g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)^{2}},

\displaystyle{\partial^{2}f\over\partial\gamma_{1}\partial\delta}

\displaystyle={1\over\gamma_{1}-\delta}+{1\over 1-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}+\delta}+{\frac{m}{n}}\cdot{\left({-2\exp\left({2\beta}\right)-2\exp\left({-2\beta}\right)+4}\right)\cdot g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)-(\partial g/\partial\gamma_{1})(\partial g/\partial\delta)\over g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)^{2}},

\displaystyle{\partial^{2}f\over\partial\gamma_{2}\partial\delta}

\displaystyle={1\over\gamma_{2}-\delta}+{1\over 1-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}+\delta}+{\frac{m}{n}}\cdot{\left({-2\exp\left({2\beta}\right)-2\exp\left({-2\beta}\right)+4}\right)\cdot g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)-(\partial g/\partial\gamma_{2})(\partial g/\partial\delta)\over g(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta)^{2}}.

In effect, the Hessian at the point
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=1/2,\delta=1/4
works out to be

\displaystyle D^{2}f\big|_{\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=1/2,\delta=1/4}=\begin{pmatrix}-8+4d\operatorname{th}\beta(\operatorname{th}(\beta)+1)&-4+4d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta&8-8d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta\\
-4+4d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta&-8+4d\operatorname{th}\beta(\operatorname{th}(\beta)+1)&8-8d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta\\
8-8d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta&8-8d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta&-16+16d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta\end{pmatrix}.

Hence,
D^{2}f\big|_{\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=1/2,\delta=1/4}
is negative definite and

\displaystyle\det D^{2}f\big|_{\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=1/2,\delta=1/4}=256\left({d\operatorname{th}^{2}\beta-1}\right)\left({d\operatorname{th}\beta-1}\right)^{2}.

Finally, let
B=[\min\mathfrak{B},\max\mathfrak{B}]
,
R=[\min\mathfrak{R}^{*},\max\mathfrak{R}^{*}]
.
Then due to (
6.41
) the Euler-Maclaurin formula shows that

\displaystyle\left|{\Sigma^{\otimes}-\int_{B}\int_{B}\int_{R}\exp\left({nh\left({{\alpha_{1}\over n},{\alpha_{2}\over n},{\rho_{++}\over n}}\right)}\right){\mathrm{d}}\alpha_{1}{\mathrm{d}}\alpha_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\rho}\right|

\displaystyle=o\left({\exp\left({nf(1/2,1/2,1/4)}\right)}\right).

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathfrak{B},\,\rho\in\mathfrak{R}^{*}}

\displaystyle\exp\left({nf\left({{\alpha_{1}\over n},{\alpha_{2}\over n},{\rho_{++}\over n}}\right)}\right)\sim\int_{B}\int_{B}\int_{R}\exp\left({nh\left({{\alpha_{1}\over n},{\alpha_{2}\over n},{\rho_{++}\over n}}\right)}\right){\mathrm{d}}\alpha_{1}{\mathrm{d}}\alpha_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\rho

\displaystyle\sim n^{3/2}\exp\left({nf(1/2,1/2,1/4)}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp\left({{1\over 2}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{1}\\
\gamma_{2}\\
\delta\end{pmatrix}^{\top}D^{2}f|_{(1/2,1/2,1/4)}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{1}\\
\gamma_{2}\\
\delta\end{pmatrix}}\right){\mathrm{d}}\gamma_{1}{\mathrm{d}}\gamma_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\delta

\displaystyle\sim\frac{{(2\pi n)^{3/2}}}{\sqrt{-\det D^{2}f|_{(1/2,1/2,1/4)}}}\exp\left({nf(1/2,1/2,1/4)}\right).

(6.42)

Combining (
6.40
) and (
6.42
) completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.21
.

The proposition follows from Corollary
6.13
and Lemma
6.14
and a bit of simplifying.
∎

6.4.
Proof of Theorem
1.2

Propositions
2.19
,
2.20
and
2.21
verify the assumptions of Theorem
3.11
for the random variable
Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)
.
Hence, Theorem
3.11
demonstrates that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)}{{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)]}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{W},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}

\displaystyle=\prod_{\ell\geq 3}(1+\chi_{\ell})^{\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}}\exp(-\vartheta_{\ell}\chi_{\ell}).

(6.43)

Here the
(\mathcal{Y}_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 3}
are independent
{\rm Po}(\vartheta_{\ell})
variables, cf. (
3.11
), and the convergence of the infinite product holds almost surely and in
L^{2}
.

In order to derive Theorem
1.2
from (
6.43
) we show that the sum

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{W}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\geq 3}\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}\log(1+\chi_{\ell})-\vartheta_{\ell}\chi_{\ell}

converges in
L^{1}
.
Indeed, since
\log(1+z)\leq z+z^{2}
and
d\operatorname{th}\beta<1
, we obtain for certain
C=C(d,\beta)>0

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}|\log\mathcal{W}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\geq 3}{\mathbb{E}}\left|{\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}\log(1+\operatorname{th}^{\ell}\beta)-\frac{d^{\ell}}{2\ell}\operatorname{th}^{\ell}\beta}\right|=\sum_{\ell\geq 3}{\mathbb{E}}\left|{\frac{d^{\ell}}{2\ell}\operatorname{th}^{\ell}\beta+\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}\frac{\operatorname{th}^{j\cdot\ell}\beta}{j}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\geq 3}{\mathbb{E}}\left|{\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}-\frac{d^{\ell}}{2\ell}}\right|\operatorname{th}^{\ell}\beta+\frac{(d\operatorname{th}\beta)^{\ell}}{2\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\operatorname{th}^{j\cdot\ell}\beta\leq C+\sum_{\ell\geq 3}\mathrm{Var}\left({\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}}\right)\operatorname{th}^{2\ell}\beta\leq C+\sum_{\ell\geq 3}(d\operatorname{th}\beta)^{\ell}<\infty.

(6.44)

Combining (
6.43
) and (
6.44
), we see that for any bounded continuous function
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[g(\log(Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0))-\log{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)])]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[{(g\circ\log)\left({\frac{Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)}{{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)]}}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[{(g\circ\log)(\mathcal{W})}\right]+o(1)={\mathbb{E}}[g(\log\mathcal{W})]+o(1).

(6.45)

Thus,
\log Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)-{\mathbb{E}}[\log Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)]
converges to
\log\mathcal{W}
in distribution.
Furthermore, Proposition
2.19
implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left|{\left({\log Z_{\mathbb{G}}(\beta,0)-\log{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{\mathbb{G}}(\beta,0)]}\right)-\left({\log Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)-\log{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{\mathbb{G},\mathrm{bal}}(\beta,0)]}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle=o(1),

whence we conclude that
\log Z_{\mathbb{G}}(\beta,0)-{\mathbb{E}}[\log Z_{\mathbb{G}}(\beta,0)]
converges to
\log\mathcal{W}
in distribution.
Finally, we obtain the explicit formula for the limiting distribution stated in Theorem
1.2
by substituting in the expression for
{\mathbb{E}}[\log Z_{\mathbb{G}}(\beta,0)]
from Proposition
2.19
.
